The 25 Best Black Friday Tech Deals — Save Up to 51% on Audio Gear, Gaming, and More

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

If you purchase an independently reviewed product or service through a link on our website, Rolling Stone may receive an affiliate commission.

It’s no secret that in recent years, Black Friday deals have typically kicked off well before the Thanksgiving turkey has even started to thaw. This year is no exception — there are already plenty of early deals on every category imaginable, including clothing, subscription services, and even travel.

Tech is always one of the biggest categories, with rare chances to nab discounts on recently released gadgets. While Black Friday officially kicks off on Nov. 27, we’ve been scouring the web to find the best tech deals that you can already take advantage of.

From headphones and speakers to computers and TVs, these are the best early Black Friday deals you can grab now.

There are a huge number of earbud, speaker, and headphone deals, including savings on top brands like Apple and Sony.

You can save $100 on the latest pro version of Apple’s AirPods, which feature great noise-canceling and a built-in heart rate sensor.

Bose’s QuietComfort headphones are available starting at $200, a massive discount for the newest model.

Sony’s XM6 headphones are among our top picks for noise-canceling, and they got a modest Black Friday discount.

Edifier’s M90 speakers are among our favorite computer speakers thanks to their versatile connectivity options and audio quality.

Sonos is running some pretty serious Black Friday deals. The Era 100 SL is the more affordable version of the 100 speaker — the difference is that the 100 SL does not have a microphone for voice control.

TVs are historically one of the biggest Black Friday categories, and this year is no different. You can save on high-end and budget models alike.

Hisense’s 4K Mini-LED TV is discounted in numerous sizes by nearly half off.

Looking to go big? You can save on Samsung’s 83-inch OLED TV.

You can pick up this 55″ TV from Best Buy’s house brand for half off.

You don’t have to be a biohacker to want to keep tabs on your health — from fitness trackers to recovery tools, there’s plenty of great health gear on sale right now.

The latest-generation Apple Watch is available starting at $300, depending on the size and color.

There are more powerful massagers out there, but make no mistake — the Theragun Mini can deliver quick relief despite its compact size.

The latest Oura ring is $100 off. It’s the smallest smart ring, meaning it won’t get in the way of your workouts. Just bear in mind that to make the most of it, you’ll need a subscription.

The Galaxy Watch8, which is the previous generation, is discounted by $100 to $249.99 for the 40mm version. It features health and fitness tracking, including blood pressure, sleep tracking, and more.

Higher Dose’s red light therapy equipment is discounted during Black Friday, including this starter kit.

You can save on PCs and computer accessories as well.

The MacBook Neo is Apple’s more affordable, entry-level laptop, and it’s even more affordable during Black Friday. The $50 discount may not be a big one, but if you’ve had your eye on the Neo, now is likely the best time to buy.

More partial to PCs? The Zenbook from Asus features a Snapdragon X series processor and is $400 off right now.

Holding out for when Grand Theft Auto VI is available on PC? This gaming monitor is nearly half off on Samsung’s website.

If you want an all-in-one solution for your desktop, Lenovo’s A100 computer is $300 off. It includes a monitor with a built-in PC, plus a wired keyboard and mouse.

Don’t forget the accessories. From chargers to smart plugs, these tools will help you make the most of your gadgets.

A USB hub is a must-have for connecting laptops to monitors, and this one features inputs for USB-C, 4K HDMI, USB-A, and Ethernet cables.

Keep misplacing your keys? You can get one AirTag for less than $20 right now.

A smart plug is one of the best ways to start building a smart home; it can be used to control fans, humidifiers, coffee makers, and more either with a voice assistant or from the app.

Nimble’s power banks are pricier than the average phone charger, but they’re made from recycled plastic and have features like fast-charging and dual charging ports.

Gamers can save on controllers for the PlayStation and XBOX, while PC accessories are discounted too.

Need a new controller for your PS5? The DualSense controller is available in a wide range of colors, and it’s $20 off at multiple retailers.

You can get a small discount on the XBOX controller, which works with the series S or X versions of the console, as well as PCs.

Prefer PC gaming? Razer’s mechanical keyboard features RGB lighting and programmable keys. It’s discounted from multiple retailers.

This gaming headset features a USB-C dongle that makes it easy to switch between multiple devices, including consoles and desktops. It’s currently $50 off.

S&P considers Nvidia credit downgrade over governance and ‘key man’ risks

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

S&P Global Ratings, one of the three major credit rating agencies, is considering downgrading Nvidia’s rating, citing concerns about the company’s management and governance practices.

Despite Nvidia’s strong financial performance, the ouster and reinstatement of CEO Jensen Huang has raised concerns about “weak executive oversight and lack of succession planning” and “key man risk,” meaning heavy reliance on Huang for its future success, S&P said in a statement.

The agency expects to finish its review within 90 days and could cut Nvidia’s rating from AA to AA-, the fourth-highest on its scale.

Although Nvidia is certain to keep its investment-grade rating, a downgrade could raised its borrowing costs. Because Nvidia backstops neoclouds and other infrastructure companies that lack strong credit on their own, a lower rating could make their debt costlier or force them to post more collateral.

In August, Nvidia announced plans to arrange $500 billion in financing for AI infrastructure with six financial institutions, including Goldman Sachs, Apollo, and Blackstone. Under the original terms, Nvidia would guarantee 25% of the loans. But even before Huang’s ouster and return, lenders had been demanding that Nvidia guarantee more, Bloomberg reported.

A downgrade would likely prompt lenders to demand even larger guarantees.

Cristian Chivu, tecnico dell'Inter, analizza ai microfoni di Sky Sport la sfida di Champions League vinta 2-0 contro lo Stoccarda: "Le partite in Champions non sono mai semplici e non le puoi sempre dominare, dobbiamo mantenere una certa organizzazione per sfruttare gli errori degli avversari ed essere propositivi. Oggi siamo stati bravi un po' in tutto, anche a difendere bene l'area sui cross. Abbiamo creato situazioni che con più lucidità e cattiveria ci potevano portare in vantaggio, alla fine l'abbiamo sbloccata e poi siamo andati sul 2-0".

Bitcoin has fallen so far that its original Rainbow Chart has run out of colors

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Bitcoin’s drop below $59,000 exposes the limitations of a valuation model built on historical price trends.

On BlockchainCenter's original Bitcoin Rainbow Chart, the price marker sat below the bottom purple band, and the chart labeled the reading “Bitcoin is dead,” as Bitcoin traded below $59,000 on Nov. 25.

On the same date, BlockchainCenter’s dynamic version displayed “BUY!”

The two readings describe the same selloff from different starting points. BlockchainCenter presents the original and dynamic charts separately, and each version measures Bitcoin against its own curve.

The original chart snapshot uses a fixed formula dating to 2014, while the dynamic chart fits its curve to daily Bitcoin prices starting in 2012.

The colored bands show where the price sits relative to each model’s estimate of its long-term trend. As the original curve rises over time, it takes a higher dollar price to reach the same band.

The dynamic model’s estimate can also change as new price history comes in, and the apparent instructions in the labels take their meaning from the curve each chart uses.

BlockchainCenter puts Bitcoin’s price against two different trends.

A price below the bands on the original chart can coexist with a buy label on the dynamic chart.

Publisher readings for November 25, 2026.

Schematic bands compare BlockchainCenter’s two published readings for November 25, 2026. The displayed dollar value belongs to the original chart snapshot.

Publisher readings for November 25, 2026.

BlockchainCenter’s weekly archive shows that the same “Bitcoin is dead” label was recorded for $74,382 on Nov. 8, $65,118 on Nov. 15, and $60,944 on Nov. 22.

Please enable JavaScript to verify and send this form.

The Nov. 25 digest also showed it, with Bitcoin below $59,000, and the label had already appeared at weekly checkpoints in May and October.

The November readings capture prices on specific dates, so they leave room for swings in between. Bitcoin can keep falling after the original chart runs out of colors, since the quoted prices capture changes the label obscures once the old chart reaches its lower limit.

In March 2020, Bitcoin briefly slipped below the chart’s lowest band during the pandemic crash, according to BlockchainCenter’s history.

It broke the old boundary again in 2022 when the price fell below $20,000. After the second break, BlockchainCenter added a band beneath its previous floor. The original formula stayed in place, and the new indigo band was labeled “1BTC = 1BTC.”

BlockchainCenter’s creator says the added band also came under pressure, so the site revised the formula in a separate V2 release on Nov. 21, 2022, using more price history to fit a new curve.

Adding a band changed how the old chart displayed low prices, and refitting the curve changed the benchmark itself. Either change could put a price that had fallen outside the old bands on the chart, even before a recovery.

The fourth Bitcoin halving in April 2024 cut the block subsidy to 3.125 BTC, offering a starting point for comparing the rally that followed with the original curve.

Bitcoin went on to set a dollar record, reaching about $126,200 on Oct. 6, 2025. Even that rally stayed short of the yellow and red bands in the portion of the original chart shown in the Nov. 25 snapshot.

Bitcoin recovered from the March 2020 shock and went on to trade well above its 2022 lows. Those recoveries show how different the market can look after a break.

In November, the original chart’s label stayed in place as the price fell, while the dynamic chart put it in a band labeled “BUY!” BlockchainCenter describes the Rainbow Chart as a way to view long-term price history.

The chart’s creator presents even the updated fit as a way to put past price moves in context.

Deere: 3 Key Takeaways for Long-Term Investors

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Deere's 2027 delivery plan hinges on flexible production schedules and labor contract talks, risks investors need to consider.

By The Motley Fool Team – Nov 25, 2026 at 5:38PM EST

10 stocks we like better than Deere & Company ›

On its fourth-quarter 2026 earnings call, held on Nov. 25, 2026, Deere (DE +4.44%) announced it believes the global agricultural equipment cycle troughed in 2026, setting up a North American-led demand recovery for fiscal year 2027. Long-term investors should care because Deere sustained 12.2% full-year equipment operating margins during a sub-trough demand environment, demonstrating far stronger earnings power than in past downturns. With dealer inventories tight and aging fleets driving replacement demand, the business remains poised to compound returns as market volumes normalize. Three things management said stand out.

North American agricultural equipment demand is rebounding as aging machinery and depleted dealer lots force farmers to replace equipment. This structural replacement cycle underpins growth even while high input costs keep farm margins tight.

Deere proved its structural profitability by delivering over 12% operating margins in Equipment Operations (Equip Ops), even while its core Production & Precision Ag (PPA) segment operated below trough levels. Disciplined cost management and inventory controls kept margins well above those of previous industry downcycles.

Deere is expanding its digital tech stack into artificial intelligence through tools like JD, which connect to its Operations Center platform. Rapid adoption across 530 million engaged acres creates recurring software value and deepens customer lock-in.

Infrastructure spending and tight dealer fleets position construction equipment for upside as non-residential projects proceed. Structural cash generation provides flexibility to invest through industry cycles while acquiring complementary technologies.

Management is maintaining flexible production build schedules and preparing contingencies for upcoming labor contract negotiations to ensure continuity of customer deliveries. Structural precision tech integration positions the business for expanding profitability as demand recovers.

China's Google Just Released Its Fast New AI Model for Free

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

China's Google Just Released Its Fast New AI Model for Free

Baidu, China's equivalent of Google, released its ERNIE 5.5 Flash Preview AI model this week, giving developers worldwide free access to a high-speed reasoning model designed to handle complex coding and autonomous tasks at relatively low computational costs.

The release comes nearly two weeks after Baidu introduced the model through its cloud computing platform. Developers can now download it, modify it and build commercial products around it under the permissive Apache 2.0 license, without paying Baidu for every interaction.

Baidu has been pursuing computational efficiency for some time. In May, the company released ERNIE 5.1, claiming its pre-training cost was just 6% of that of comparable AI models.

You can now act on the news you read: Go long or short Anthropic, Nvidia, Google and more with perpetual futures, all from your Decrypt Money Account.

ERNIE 5.5 Flash Preview uses a mixture-of-experts architecture, which divides the neural network into specialized subnetworks. The model has 280 billion parameters—the numerical values that encode what an AI learned during training—but uses only 14 billion to process each token.

Baidu also compressed most of the model's weights to four-bit precision to reduce memory consumption and incorporated a technique called speculative decoding, which drafts several tokens in parallel for the main model to verify. Its context window exceeds one million tokens, allowing it to process hundreds of thousands of words within a single session.

In one demonstration, Baidu tasked the model with developing a third-person martial arts video game using Unreal Engine 5.8. The model spent 11 hours writing code, integrating existing graphical assets and iteratively fixing bugs, despite being unable to process images natively.

On Terminal-Bench 4.0, which measures an AI agent's ability to complete computer tasks through a command-line interface, ERNIE achieved 46.5%. DeepSeek V4.1 Flash scored 42.3%, GLM-5.3-Flash achieved 35.8%, and OpenAI's GPT-6.1 Sol scored 56.1%.

The Chinese model also outperformed both GPT-6.1 Sol and Claude Opus 5.5 on AutomationBench, which evaluates business workflows across different applications. ERNIE scored 61.9%, compared with 49.3% for GPT and 58% for Claude, according to Baidu's evaluations.

These are manufacturer-reported results, however, and the evaluations use different software environments for some competing models.

Baidu's international API pricing is $0.28 per million input tokens and $1.13 per million output tokens. The model is also available through a free tier on OpenRouter.

The decision to release the model puts Baidu alongside Chinese competitors like Alibaba and DeepSeek, which have made increasingly capable AI models available for businesses to run on their own infrastructure.

For Baidu, giving away a model could help attract developers to an ecosystem where the company makes money selling computing resources. Its AI cloud infrastructure revenue grew 94% year over year in the third quarter, helping offset weakness in its advertising business.

The model is technically open-weight rather than fully open-source AI, since the release does not provide everything necessary to reproduce its training. Running it independently also requires substantial hardware: Even its most compressed official version contains approximately 166 GB of model files.

Baidu says the model is still undergoing training and has yet to announce a date for the final release of ERNIE 5.5 Flash.

Rockstar recreated an embarrassing mishap in Florida infrastructure.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

When you're exploring a world as realistic and well-observed as GTA 6's Leonida, it's rare for a piece of benign infrastructure to stick out. But on night one of my playthrough, I clocked some weird streetlights.

A stylistic choice by Rockstar? Maybe, but it's awfully specific, and I had a feeling there was a deeper intention behind it. So I DMed my buddy Henry, an honest-to-god Floridian who was also playing GTA 6, and asked what's up. At first he thought strange streetlights in the Key Lento area of the map were special lights meant to not disorient turtles, but those are typically red, so probably not. And that doesn't explain the purple ones.

So Henry reached out to friends in south Florida, the region that Leonida's biggest cities are based on, and they cracked the case wide open.

"The purple [lights] are definitely real. We have a ton of them down here because whoever is in charge of that in the government bought a bunch of defective lights," the anonymous Floridian responded.

Yes, actual Florida cities are lit by these dubiously dim, yet undeniably cool purple lamps. According to ClickOrlando, residents first started reporting the purple streetlights in 2023. They put up hundreds of these things across the state before they started malfunctioning—it was such a kerfuffle that the government eventually released a statement saying their vendor really screwed the pooch.

Keep up to date with the most important stories and the best deals, as picked by the PC Gamer team.

Apparently the cause was a "spectral shift caused by phosphor displacement," which essentially means the LED lights were always blue, but the coating that was supposed to make appear yellow or white or whatever didn't work. The powers that be said the malfunctioning lights would be replaced, but judging by our own sources in the state, they haven't gotten them all.

So that's fun. If you already heard about this somewhere (other than your Floridian friend), it's probably because YouTuber AnyAustin also clocked these lights in his video published yesterday. Glad I'm not the only one who couldn't resist this rabbit hole.

I like how Rockstar didn't go overboard with these lights—just like the genuine article, they're in and around parts of Leonida, but not everywhere. I'm still not sure how to explain the turquoise ones, though. A lot of local news, Reddit, and YouTube skimming did not produce a clear answer, other than other cities in America that do have green streetlights sometimes. Perhaps Key Lento has lamps busted in a slightly different way, or it's just a calming shade of blue that Rockstar thought looked nice. It does, to be fair.

If you've played over a hundred hours of Vampire Survivors you've probably forgotten how slow its early progression feels. It certainly seemed that way based on the reaction to Warhammer Survivors from Vampire Survivors veterans, who complained about its grindiness compared to their beloved bullet heaven originator, despite the arc of both games being quite similar. Developers Auroch Digital made Warhammer Survivors in partnership with Vampire Survivors creator Poncle, so of course the two games have a lot in common.

Other complaints were more valid. The Beasts of Nurgle did have too many hit points, and their ability to regenerate only exacerbated that. The treasure chest-opening animation did take a bit too long. And some of the weapons were undertuned. Fortunately, those issues and more have been addressed in hotfix v2.18. Here are the highlights:

The change to XP is an interesting one. It's a standard part of the genre's formula to have random XP drops—killing some enemies gives you nothing, while others drop XP gems and tempt you to rush into a mob to grab them. Making every enemy drop at least a low-value XP gem will speed up leveling a bit, but hopefully when you see green or red gems you'll still want to risk a mob of orks to yoink.

Auroch Digital recently revealed a roadmap of upcoming additions to Warhammer Survivors, with the first big update in December promising more characters, weapons, and weapon evolutions. January's updates will add more quality-of-life and balance improvements, as well as cross-save, with another update in the first quarter of 2027 bringing another round of characters, weapons, and weapon evolutions.

The final item on the roadmap is a tease that simply says, "ERROR… Details classified." It's probably not the addition of a whole extra universe to its current Warhammer 40,000 and Age of Sigmar settings, but I'd be stoked to see them add the other popular world of Warhammer. By which I mean Blood Bowl, of course.

It’s a solid option for movie enthusiasts and gamers alike, thanks to its self-lit pixels that produce deep blacks and excellent contrast, particularly in darker scenes where conventional LED TVs can struggle with a grayish glow. For a more affordable 75-inch option, Hisense's 2026 U6 Pro is down to $699.99 from $1,399.99, saving you $700. Like the U7, it uses mini-LED backlighting to improve contrast and bring out more detail in HDR movies and shows, although it sits a tier below the U7 in terms of overall picture performance. It combines excellent pixel-level contrast with gaming features designed for fast-paced titles, making it a versatile choice for both movies and consoles. LG also supports Dolby Vision, which gives it an advantage over Samsung's OLEDs for viewers who regularly stream compatible HDR content.

Sony’s 65-inch Bravia 6, the entry-level OLED in its 2026 TV lineup, is now $1,198, down from $1,999.99, saving you $802. It delivers the deep blacks and excellent contrast you'd expect from the display technology, thanks to its self-lit pixels. Sony's picture processing also helps bring out fine details and natural-looking colors in movies and TV shows, while Dolby Vision support makes the most of compatible HDR content.

For the same $1,198, you can get a considerably larger screen with the 85-inch Sony Bravia 3 II, which is down from $1,599.99, saving you $402. This 2026 model uses a conventional LED panel, so it won't quite match the Bravia 6's deep blacks and contrast, but the extra 20 inches make it an appealing option for sports and family movie nights. It also has a native 120Hz refresh rate with VRR and ALLM for smoother console gaming, along with Dolby Vision support and Google TV with Gemini built in.

That also makes it a good pick for a bright living room, where an OLED might not be the most practical choice. You also get a native 144Hz refresh rate for gaming and Fire TV built in

Roku’s 65-inch Plus Series is the most affordable TV in this roundup at $449.99, down from $649.99, saving you $200. This 2025 model combines a QLED panel with mini-LED backlighting and Dolby Vision support for improved contrast and richer colors when watching HDR content. LG’s 65-inch C6 OLED has the largest dollar discount in this sale at $1,350 off, which brings it from $2,699.99 down to $1,349.99.

There's no guarantee that Cyber Monday will bring lower TV prices, particularly when some of the Black Friday deals are already offering discounts approaching 50%. Retailers sometimes carry the same promotions through the entire shopping weekend, while others introduce new discounts or change which models are on sale.

That depends on your budget, room size, and how you plan to use it. The LG OLED evo C6 and Samsung S90H are worth considering for movie enthusiasts and gamers who want excellent contrast and smooth motion, while the Hisense U7 and TCL QM64L offer mini-LED alternatives for brighter living rooms. For a large screen at a relatively affordable price, the 75-inch Hisense U6 Pro at $699.99 stands out, while the 65-inch Roku Plus Series at $449.99 makes sense for everyday streaming on a tighter budget. If screen size is your priority, the 98-inch TCL F8D is also worth a look, provided you have the room for it.

Choosing between OLED and QLED is an important decision you need to ask yourself before you go shopping for TVs. If you can afford an OLED and want the best image possible, go for the OLED. Just make sure you're okay with the dimmer lights they emit. If you need brightness and don't want to blow out your budget on a TV, go with a QLED.

Medical Costs Are Deductible Only Above 7.5% of Income – Self-Employed Premiums Skip That Rule

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Many or all of the credit card, banking, and other offers on this site are from companies that pay us. For example, we may be paid when you click a link, are approved for a product, or open an account. Compensation may influence which products we cover and how, where, and in what order they appear. It doesn’t influence our editorial evaluations, ratings, or opinions, which are ours alone. We don’t include every bank, credit card company, or offer available in the marketplace.

You can deduct only the medical and dental costs above 7.50% of your adjusted gross income, your income minus a few adjustments, and only if you itemize. Count the health premiums you pay yourself, Medicare’s included. If you’re self-employed and can’t join an employer’s plan, deduct your premiums separately, with no threshold or itemizing, up to your business’s profit.

For a retiree with $60,000 of adjusted gross income, the threshold is $4,500. With $9,000 of medical costs, Medicare premiums included, she can deduct the $4,500 above it if itemizing beats her standard deduction, which is bigger for anyone 65 or older.

Count what you paid out of pocket for doctors, hospitals, dentists, prescriptions, glasses, and hearing aids, plus the miles you drive to appointments at the IRS’s medical mileage rate. Premiums count too, including Medicare Part B and Part D, coverage you buy yourself, and qualified long-term care insurance up to an IRS limit set by age.

Nonprescription medicine other than insulin, most cosmetic surgery, and gym dues for general fitness don’t count. Neither do premiums your employer takes out of your pay before tax, or bills you pay from a health savings account, since that money was never taxed. IRS Publication 502 lists the rest.

If you’re self-employed, premiums for health coverage you buy on your own go on Schedule 1 instead, where they lower your adjusted gross income without a threshold or itemizing. Medicare premiums count there too, but the deduction can’t exceed your business’s profit. Any month you could join a subsidized plan through your job or your spouse’s doesn’t qualify, and premiums the deduction doesn’t cover can count with your other medical costs.

Costs count in the year you pay them, and a credit card charge counts in the year you make it, even if you pay off the card later. Say the retiree’s income stays at $60,000 and her costs run $6,000 a year. That clears her $4,500 threshold by $1,500 each year, or $3,000 over two years. The same $12,000 paid in one year clears it by $7,500, and that larger total is likelier to beat her standard deduction.

Once this year’s costs get close to your threshold, have the dental work, hearing aids, or eye surgery you’ve been putting off done before December 31, and pay the bill by then.

Before you file, compare the best free tax software to see which free version covers your return.

In vista dell'imminente sfida di domani sera tra Olympiacos e Milan, mister Amorim ha voluto commentare la gara e il rendimento dei rossoneri ai microfoni di Sky Sport. Questo un breve estratto del suo discorso sulla mentalità del Milan, sempre competitiva in ogni situazione:

Attacker used 1Password employee’s GitHub account to copy customer records

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Identity security company 1Password today disclosed a security incident in which an attacker, working through an employee’s corporate GitHub account, copied account records belonging to a subset of its customers.

According to the company, the incident came to light on Nov. 19, when 1Password began investigating signs that the GitHub account had been compromised. Using the account, the attacker is said to have pushed malicious code to 1Password’s backend services, which then downloaded what the company describes as “administrative data pertaining to our customers.”

The records taken include names and email addresses, account and user identifiers, account creation and last sign-in times, public keys, Secure Remote Password protocol parameters and other non-secret account metadata. Rounding out the list are tokens found in certain emailed links for invitations, provisioning and account recovery.

Customer vaults, the items stored in them and the keys that protect them were not affected, according to 1Password. “At no time were customer vault contents accessed by, or available to, the attacker,” the company said in its disclosure. Account passwords and Secret Keys are not stored by the company. Also left untouched, the company says, were account recovery keys and the 1Password apps and browser extensions.

Beyond the customer records, the attacker is said to have copied source code from private repositories. Access was also gained to 1Password’s continuous integration and continuous delivery system, which contains infrastructure-related secrets needed for its product to work. According to 1Password, the source code contains no user secrets. Having triggered a rotation of all credentials that may have been exposed, the company claims there has been no impact to its services, availability or product security.

The tokens taken from emailed links cannot be used on their own to access an account, since completing an invitation or recovery also requires a sign-in through single sign-on or a six-digit code sent to the user’s registered email address. Users with provisioning invites or recovery requests pending at the time of the incident, said to be a small number, have been notified directly.

As a precaution, the emailed codes have now been lengthened from six numeric digits to 10 alphanumeric characters. 1Password says it has found no internal evidence of account takeovers using the exposed tokens. Other steps taken in response include disabling the affected GitHub account, revoking the stolen token, tightening internal controls and bringing in independent cybersecurity experts to validate its containment and remediation work.

Customers do not need to change anything in their 1Password accounts. The company is instead warning users to be cautious of phishing attempts, such as unexpected emails, texts or calls about invitations or account recovery requests they did not start. “1Password will never contact you to ask for your account password or Secret Key,” the company noted.

1Password, which claims to serve more than 200,000 businesses, did not say how many customers had records downloaded. How the employee’s GitHub account was compromised was also left unexplained.

This is not the first intrusion 1Password has disclosed. In October 2023, the company said that an attacker had accessed its Okta tenant using session data stolen in the breach of Okta Inc.’s customer support system. No user data was found to have been compromised in that case.

Today’s disclosure comes nearly four years after rival password manager LastPass US LP revealed that a hacker had copied encrypted customer password vaults, using information taken in an earlier breach of its development environment.

1Password says it will update its post if the investigation turns up anything that changes what it has disclosed so far.

Controversial Senate Bill Would Speed Up Development on Public Lands

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

On November 19, the United State Senate passed a sweeping 400-page bill with big implications for America’s federally managed public lands. Called the Bipartisan American Affordability and Jobs Act—or BAJAA for short—the bill would ease permitting restrictions for all sorts of public-land development projects, from mining and drilling to large-scale solar farms and geothermal power projects. Supporters say it would open the door for more clean-energy projects on public lands while reducing utility costs for American consumers.

Co-sponsored by Sen. Martin Heinrich (D-NM) and Sen. Mike Lee (R-UT), BAJAA has drawn widespread criticism from conservationists, environmentalists, and public land advocates, with groups from the Wilderness Society to the National Wildlife Federation speaking out in opposition. According to the National Council of American Indians, it has also caused serious concerns in tribal nations across the country.

A large portion of the bill aims to reform or streamline the permitting process that developers must adhere to when applying for projects on federal public lands. It would do so by shrinking the scope, timelines, and legal challenges currently available under both the National Environmental Policy Act (NEPA) and the Clean Water Act (CWA).

In an October blog post, the Theodore Roosevelt Conservation Partnership (TRCP) said “permitting reform rules decide how a pipeline, transmission line, mine or dam gets approved, whether hunters and anglers have any say in where it goes—and what happens to elk winter range, trout streams or migration corridors in their path.”

While TRCP has stopped short of publicly opposing the bill, they did highlight several concerns in the post. For starters, they flagged a provision in the bill—Section 1401—that “would make it much more challenging to fix problems once a project is permitted.”

Titled “Project Certainty,” this section of the BAJAA bill would restrict federal agencies—like the Bureau of Land Management or the US Forest Service—from revoking previously issued permits during future presidential administrations. Critics say this section of the bill could lock in controversial projects—like a proposed copper-sulfide mine on the edge of the Boundary Waters Canoe Area Wilderness or the 211-mile Ambler mining road that the Department of the Interior (DOI) is pushing for Alaska’s Brooks Range.

In a separate blog post, Backcountry Hunters & Anglers (BHA) highlighted similar concerns with the “Project Certainty” provision in the BAJAA bill. “For BHA, this raises questions about whether federal land managers would retain sufficient flexibility to respond to changing conditions and protect public resources over the long term,” the BHA post states. “For example, if a mining project near Minnesota’s Boundary Waters Canoe Area Wilderness received federal approval, these provisions could limit a future administration’s ability to revisit that approval in response to newly identified impacts on water quality or fisheries, depending on the circumstances and applicable legal exceptions.”

Aaron Kindle is the Director of Sporting Advocacy at the National Wildlife Federation (NWF). He tells Field & Stream that NWF opposes the BAJAA bill in its current form because it raises too many questions, is moving too fast without adequate public awareness, and it undermines key public-land safeguards.

“This is a hugely consequential bill happening during a lame-duck session with very little public understanding,” Kindle says. “[NWF] is pro-permitting reform. But when we do the analysis on this, and we talk to other conservation friends and partners, it’s just too fraught.”

Kindle says NWF shares both BHA’s and TRCP’s concerns around the bill’s project certainty guarantees for developers. “It shortens review timelines, it narrows what triggers a review, and it kind of locks it in once it’s done,” he says. “We’ve always wanted to be able to do adaptive management on anything that’s done. Anytime you narrow environmental review parameters like this, or make permits too concrete, that’s concerning for a broad swath of our most cherished public lands and waters.”

Kindle says NWF is also worried that the BAJAA bill could expedite data center construction on public lands. Up until now, no data center has been permitted on any federal public lands, though the DOI did attempt to approve one on BLM lands in Nevada back in August, before a federal appeals court revoked the permit.

“What we’ve understood so far is that [BAJAA] shields [data center developers] from future regulations and makes it hard, or maybe even impossible, to revoke the permits once they’ve been awarded,” he adds. “What if we learn at some future point that these things are incredibly harmful to water, air, wildlife, the grid, who knows—and then they’re just locked in place, and there’s nothing we can do about it?”

The Wilderness Society has been one of the most vocal opponents of the BAJAA bill since it first started making headlines in early November. Wilderness Society President, Tracy Stone-Manning, who ran the BLM during the Biden Administration, calls the bill alarming.

“Some of the places that hang in the balance are the Boundary Waters in northern Minnesota and the Brooks Range of Alaska, because of the proposed Ambler Road and Arctic Mine, the entire Alaskan Arctic really, and all of that caribou habitat,” Stone-Manning tells F&S. “You’ve also got places in the Lower 48, like the Roan Plateau in Colorado with its amazing mule deer and elk herds, which sportsmen and women fought so hard to protect, and the headwaters of Montana’s Bitterroot River, where they want to build a rare-earth mine up on Sheep Creek. Whatever the Trump Administration decides to do in these places in the next two years, that gets locked in if this bill passes. We won’t be able to undo it.”

The Wilderness Society supports expanded permitting for renewable energy projects on public lands, which have declined steeply during the first two years of the Trump Administration. But for Stone-Manning, the risks of the BAJAA bill far outweigh the rewards. “The bottom line is, this bill takes away environmental safeguards for habitat and gives developers certainty,” she says. “Those two things coupled together spell disaster for our country’s conservation heritage and for our public-land hunting and fishing traditions.”

Many conservationists who opposes BAJAA are confused by Sen. Martin Heinrich’s staunch support for the bill. Heinrich has been one of the Senate’s most stalwart defenders of public lands for years. He has helped secure Wilderness Area and National Monument status for thousands of acres of public lands in his home state of New Mexico, for example. More recently, he was a vocal and outspoken opponent of an attempt to sell off more than 3.4 million acres of Western public lands in the “One Big Beautiful Bill.”

Today, he’s defending BAJAA in the halls of Congress and in the op-ed pages of major news outlets like the New York Times and the Economist. In a recent interview with Field & Stream, Heinrich cited an urgent need to slow climate change as his primary motivator for helping craft and co-sponsoring the legislation.

“This will allow us to put green energy on the grid at a scale where we might be able to mitigate the worst future outcomes of climate change,” Heinrich says. “I’ve realized that I’m watching the world change at such a pace, that if we don’t get to work on this, I don’t know what kind of world my grandkids are going to inherit. The places that I fish in New Mexico, for example, are changing. We don’t have a lot of water. If these places go away, and we don’t have snowpack, we’re done. It’s game over.”

Heinrich says the bill address climate change by putting public land renewable projects—like wind, solar, and geothermal—on equal footing with oil, gas, and mining. “We want to bring renewables into the same regulatory permitting regime that the fossil fuel projects already have,” he says. “Right now, oil and gas in particular, and natural gas pipelines, have a sort of privileged position.”

Heinrich disputes claims from his critics that the bill weakens key public-land safeguards like NEPA and the Clean Water Act. And he says it won’t expedite or lock-in mining permits in the Boundary Waters.

“We don’t change or lower standards in this bill; we streamline the process,” he says. “If you’re a mine developer, you still have to get your Clean Water Act permit, and it doesn’t change the standard for that. When you look at the Boundary Waters, what I don’t see is that project being able to clear is a state Clean Water Act permit, because I just don’t think it will be possible to do mining open-air tailings at that site—having seen it with my own eyes—without the acid generated making it into the water next door.”

Heinrich also disagrees with BAJAA opponents who say the bill will open the door for data center development on public lands. “I don’t think that data centers belong on public lands, and I think any effort to build them on public lands is probably a legal stretch of existing authorizations and presidential authorities,” he adds. “This bill just makes data centers pay their fair freight for attaching to the grid, instead of passing that cost on to everyday consumers in the form higher electricity rates.”

The Trump Administration’s current policy around data centers is murky at best. But recent reporting from the Washington Sun revealed that the BLM was reviewing at least 12 data center proposals spread across 17,600 acres of public land in six western states. In its recent strategic plan, the Department of the Interior, which manages close to 500 million acres of public land, listed data center development as a top priority, alongside the potential transfer or sale of federal public lands to states and local governments.

Sen. Ron Wyden (D-OR) tried to attach an amendment to the bill that would have banned the construction of data centers on public lands altogether when the Senate was debating BAJAA earlier this month. That effort ultimately failed and Wyden voted against the bill, though it still passed the full Senate on 63-33 vote.

President Trump endorsed the BAJAA bill in a recent Truth Social post and said he’ll sign it if it comes across his desk, calling it a big win for energy independence and dominance. Now its awaiting a vote in the US House of Representatives. House members could vote as early as next week, when they return from Thanksgiving recess.

Travis is Field & Stream's full-time associate editor. He covers breaking outdoor news and pressing conservation issues.

ChargePoint and PistenBully have a charging plan for ski resorts

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The next EV to plug in at a ski resort might be the one that groomed your trail. ChargePoint has struck a deal with Kässbohrer, the German company behind PistenBully snow groomers, to bring charging to ski resorts and indoor ski facilities across Europe and North America.

Under the agreement, Kässbohrer will connect its customers with ChargePoint for chargers, software, and support with planning, installation, and day-to-day operations. Resorts can use the same provider for the snow groomers and other vehicles they keep at the maintenance depot, as well as for the EV chargers skiers use while they’re out on the slopes.

Kässbohrer’s first fully electric groomer, the PistenBully 100 E, uses electric motors to move the machine and spin the tiller that smooths the snow. It has a 196 kWh battery and can handle up to three hours of normal grooming on a charge, according to the manufacturer. It can take up to 22 kW on AC or 150 kW on a DC fast charger. It fast charges from zero to 100% in about an hour.

ChargePoint says Kässbohrer accounts for more than half of the snow groomers sold worldwide each year. Resorts can start working with the companies now. Which resorts will install ChargePoint’s chargers first, and how many will go in, are TBC.

CoreWeave Stock Could Soar 125%, According to One Wall Street Analyst, Even After a Price Target Cut. Is It a Buy?

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Two analysts just trimmed their CoreWeave targets but stayed bullish. The company's bonds are sending a more cautious message.

By Jonathan Ponciano – Nov 25, 2026 at 5:25PM EST

10 stocks we like better than CoreWeave ›

CoreWeave (CRWV -0.08%) has had a brutal month. The AI cloud provider, which rents out Nvidia-powered computing capacity to tech giants, closed at $39.51 on Nov. 20, below its $40 IPO price from March 2025. It briefly rebounded above $48 on Monday, then gave most of that back. At about $42.57 as of Wednesday's market close, the stock is down roughly 70% from its 52-week high of $138.25 and down nearly 77% from its all-time closing high.

Wall Street analysts, meanwhile, are lowering their expectations, but not by much, and the gap between the bulls and the market is the crux of the debate.

Cantor Fitzgerald on Wednesday lowered its price target to $96 from $100 and kept its overweight rating. The firm said fears about CoreWeave's business model "continue to be overblown" and told clients to "be thankful for the discount the market has given them." Cantor said the cut reflects a higher expected share count, because raising equity at a lower stock price means issuing more shares.

A day earlier, Evercore ISI cut its target to $80 from $100 and kept its outperform rating. The analyst said the sharp correction in AI stocks has put more focus on pricing and downside risks, and assessed where the sell-off "might have overextended."

From Wednesday's closing price, Cantor's target implies upside of about 125%, and Evercore's implies about 89%.

CoreWeave's lenders are far less relaxed. Its notes due October 2031 fell nearly 2 cents to about 74 cents on the dollar on Wednesday, pushing their yield to 17.91%, Reuters reported, citing Tradeweb data. Its notes due June 2030 fell to a record low of 76.55 cents. The cost of insuring CoreWeave's debt against default has also climbed to levels usually seen at distressed companies.

A yield near 18% is a warning sign. It means lenders are demanding a return closer to what you'd expect from a stock, and bondholders get paid before shareholders. CoreWeave does have a cheaper way to borrow: its banks are preparing to sell a $4.5 billion loan backed by chips it is installing for Meta Platforms (META +3.40%) as soon as next week, Bloomberg reported. Because Meta is the customer, that loan is expected to be rated investment-grade and priced around 7% to 7.5%. But that kind of financing only works for capacity tied to a specific customer contract, not for the company as a whole.

Even Cantor concedes the market is "extremely focused" on one issue: CoreWeave's need for as much as $5 billion of "parent capital" in 2027, meaning money raised at the company level to support its build-out. Cantor thinks management should address the funding plan "sooner rather than later" and said a clear answer could act as a "potential clearing event" for the stock.

That's the crux. If CoreWeave raises capital through new shares at today's prices, existing shareholders are diluted. If it borrows, it pays rates the bond market has just pushed sharply higher. Either route makes it harder to reach the upside in those price targets.

For most investors, not yet. The analysts may well be right that the sell-off overshot, and Cantor says its review of how CoreWeave resigns customer contracts gives it more confidence in the model. But when the company's own bonds yield nearly 18%, the market is pricing in real risk that shareholders would absorb first.

Risk-tolerant investors who believe in long-term AI demand could start a small position, but the smarter move is to wait for the 2027 funding plan Cantor is asking for. If management lays out a credible way to raise that money without heavy dilution, the stock could rerate quickly. Until then, there are less leveraged ways to own the AI build-out among cloud stocks and semiconductor stocks.

When our analyst team has a stock tip, it can pay to listen. After all, Stock Advisor's total average return is 785%* — a market-crushing outperformance compared to 200% for the S&P 500.

They just revealed what they believe are the 10 best stocks for investors to buy right now, available when you join Stock Advisor.

The Ideal Thanksgiving Place Setting for Every Member of the Family

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The only thing that feels more like Thanksgiving than an exquisitely burnished roast turkey is the (sometimes chaotic) assemblage of loved ones seated around it. For your tablescape this year, take inspiration from the beautifully unique cast of characters that make a family a family.

She just stepped off a red eye from the big city, but she looks like she came from a gallery opening—and her place setting is as bold and eclectic as her fashion sense. Bulbous postmodern ceramic plates, '90s Art Deco revival flatware from Japan, and a hand-beaded placemat are exactly the things she’d be into. The button-shaped aluminum coaster is seldom occupied, since she prefers to keep the martini glass right where it belongs: in her hand.

She’s 25 going on 70. In between baking all of the pies for the big dinner, she learned lace-making from a TikTok tutorial. She sews, she antiques, and she self-publishes Jane Austen fanfic online. The cheeky cotton bottle apron and pearl-tipped doily on her table might be brand new from Gohar World, but they pair exquisitely with the pewter flatware she scored at an estate sale. Quilted floral placemats and plates adorned with dainty, hand-painted bows from The Six Bells, a store leading the revival of country cottage decor, evoke the pastoral, bucolic lifestyle she yearns for (as long as she can keep her 10-step skin-care routine).

Herzerlmuster Scalloped Placemats (Set of 2)

The combo of unbridled energy and still-developing motor skills means the Gen-Alpha kids are best served by a spread of disposable and shatterproof items. And, seeing that it’ll take some clever coaxing to pull these little gremlins away from their screens, their table must match the intense digital stimuli they’re accustomed to. Loop-de-loop silly straws, melamine plates, and acrylic forks and cups in as many colors as possible are the bare minimum to keep minds forged in a Cocomelon crucible engaged for the total duration of a Thanksgiving meal. Despite the visual chaos, their table might be more tranquil than the adults’.

Prue Leith Melamine Dinnerware Set and Tumblers

Thanksgiving might be the only time you see this elusive individual, as he otherwise spends the year fussing around his DIY Earthship in the desert outside of Santa Fe. His place setting is filled with natural materials, like these Shigaraki-style earthenware plates and zero-waste Belgian linen placemats. If he starts defending his dedication to composting toilets a little too enthusiastically, fill his hand-carved wooden mug with some herbal tea and he’ll settle down fine.

A matriarch par excellence, she’s been holding the family together for decades. Her demeanor might be as breezy as a Big Sur afternoon, but she means business when it comes to hosting Thanksgiving. Let it be known: She had a Nancy Meyers kitchen long before Nancy Meyers picked up a camera. To steal her vibe, invest in an Italian table cloth festooned with vines, hand-painted ceramic wicker plates, and bamboo-stemmed wine glasses. Place cards, arranged for maximum harmony, should bear each name in handwritten cursive. Miss Manners would be so proud.

Bamboo Balloon Crystal Glasses (Set of 2)

If you’re looking for a comprehensive live TV and on-demand streaming service, check out DirecTV. The service is currently offering a five-day free trial and introductory discounts on all of its packages.

STARZ is heavily discounted for Black Friday. The service, which is normally $11.99/month or $69.99/year, is running a deal that gets you a year-long subscription for the price of a month ($12).

A good VPN is essential if you’re looking to stream content that’s not available in your physical location. One of our favorites is Proton VPN, which is offering up to 70% off for its one-year plan right now.

Capcom Is Officially Killing Its 'Jurassic World Meets Overwatch' Shooter

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Four years after the game's launch, Capcom is officially shutting down Exoprimal, the company's 5v5 action game about a futuristic dinosaur crisis. Capcom announced Tuesday that service for Exoprimal will be discontinued in September 2027 — meaning that Exoprimal will no longer be playable — and that the game will no longer be sold starting in March 2027.

While disappointing for stalwart Exoprimal fans, the writing has been on the wall since 2024, when Capcom all but confirmed the sci-fi action game's grim fate.

In an official notice posted to the Exoprimal website, Capcom confirms that players will lose access to the game next year. "We regret to announce that service for Exoprimal will be discontinued as of 8:00 PM (PDT) on Wednesday, September 29, 2027," the shutdown announcement reads. "We cannot thank you enough for the love and support you have shown over the years, and we sincerely apologize for the gravity of this announcement."

Capcom has already pulled the game's four seasonal Survival Passes from stores, and warns that Exoprimal, its DLC, and all related products will no longer be sold on digital stores as of March 30, 2027. The game will shut down completely on Sept. 29, 2027, as Exoprimal is first and foremost a team-based competitive multiplayer game. Capcom notes that it will still be possible to download the game after Exoprimal is removed from stores.

Exoprimal was released in July 2023 on PlayStation 4, PlayStation 5, Windows PC, Xbox One, and Xbox Series X. The game is a multiplayer third-person shooter in which players control an Exofighter — an exosuit-clad soldier — who is tasked with completing objectives while also fighting hordes of dinosaurs in an attempt to defeat the evil artificial intelligence Leviathan. Players are grouped into two teams of five and compete against each other while battling ancient life and futuristic AI.

Capcom announced in July 2024 that, after four seasons of content, Exoprimal players would subsist on recycled, rotating seasons — all but confirming that the publisher was ready to put the new IP behind it.

Exoprimal was released to mixed reviews in 2023. In Polygon's review of the game, which drew comparisons to Overwatch, Dino Crisis, and the Earth Defense Force games, we criticized the game's opening hours, which make "a terrible first impression." But, as reviewer Jordan Oloman wrote, Exoprimal has some surprises in store several hours into the experience.

"This paradigm shift changes Exoprimal considerably, introducing new maps, a healthy difficulty spike, raid bosses, and, at long last, some genuine narrative intrigue," Oloman wrote. "It is a profoundly confusing pacing decision where you’re abruptly playing a very different, arguably much better game several hours in, one that actually sets a fascinating precedent for future multiplayer storytelling shenanigans. In the game’s universe, this is canonized as a 'firmware update' — which makes sense, but I just wish it would have come much sooner, or was at least communicated better in the game’s marketing, as many players might not gain access to Exoprimal’s best assets before bouncing off of its boring early hours."

Exoprimal has a "mostly positive" user review rating on Steam, based on more than 1,700 English-language reviews. Recent reviews, particularly those submitted after the shutdown notice, have been much more negative.

Capcom’s nostalgic romp through the halcyon days of hero shooters has pacing issues

I know a lot of people are wondering whether Black Friday deals are any good this year, and so far, I've been pleasantly surprised. I've been covering Black Friday sales as a journalist since 2019, and this year, I've found discounts of 50% and up on flagship LG OLED TVs, noise-cancelling headphones from Bose, and new Amazon Echo products.

2 Billionaire Funds Dumped AMD Last Quarter. Is It a Sell?

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

AMD's stock is pricey compared to its peers.

By Keithen Drury – Nov 25, 2026 at 5:23PM EST

10 stocks we like better than Advanced Micro Devices ›

AMD (AMD -1.75%) has been a fantastic stock to own in 2026. The stock has risen around 100% so far, though it was up 200% at the start of October. AMD became a popular choice for many investors, including the smart money in hedge funds. However, now that the latest round of 13-F filings has come out, it's clear that selling AMD has become a trend among fund managers.

Two major funds sold all of their shares during the third quarter: Appaloosa Management, run by David Tepper, and Coatue Management, run by Philippe Laffont. When giant funds like this sell, it's normally time to start thinking about selling your shares as well. So, is it time to move on from AMD stock?

First, let's tackle the business. AMD has always played second fiddle to Nvidia (NVDA -3.91%) in the data center industry, as Nvidia's hardware and software have always been a step ahead of AMD's. However, AMD's products are becoming more popular among certain customers because they are cheaper. While they may not be as capable, power-efficient, or user-friendly, they can be quite useful if they’re not being tasked with a wide variety of operations.

This has led to more adoption of AMD's products, and AMD's Q3 results back that up. The company experienced 97% year-over-year revenue growth in its data center division to $8.5 billion during Q3. However, AMD is more than just a data center company. Its overall growth rate was 45% year-over-year to $11.5 billion, as its client and gaming segment decreased year-over-year, partially offset by increasing Embedded processors doing well. Overall, those are solid results for AMD, but there's just one problem: It's far smaller than Nvidia.

AMD already has a tough enough time being compared to Nvidia's products. For it to be a better investment than Nvidia, it should be showing stronger growth, but it's not. During its fiscal third quarter (ended in October), Nvidia's overall revenue rose 103% year over year to nearly $116 billion. So, Nvidia is 10 times larger than AMD. On the data center side, Nvidia's revenue rose 110% to $108 billion.

Nvidia has the technology edge and the growth edge. However, AMD has dramatically outperformed Nvidia this year, and there is one obvious reason for that: valuation.

Sometimes, a stock goes up not because the business is doing well, but because the market is willing to pay more for the stock. That's exactly what's going on here, and it has gotten a bit out of control with AMD's stock.

AMD is one of the pricier stocks on the market, trading at 81 times trailing earnings and 21 times next year’s earnings.

Because 13F filings don't reveal exact transaction dates, we don't know whether these funds exited early in Q3 or right before the quarter ended—when AMD traded at over 80 times forward earnings and nearly 40 times next year's earnings—though either way they missed the stock's early-October peak.

Still, when you compare price tags, AMD is far more expensive, because Nvidia trades at 15 times trailing earnings and 8.6 times next year's earnings. Nvidia is clearly the far better value, and with it growing faster at a much larger scale, Nvidia’s stock looks like a far better investment.

So, while now may not be the best time to sell AMD compared to a month and a half ago, I still think investors would be wise to move on to another AI stock. Many artificial intelligence investments are on huge sale right now, but AMD is not one of them.

When our analyst team has a stock tip, it can pay to listen. After all, Stock Advisor's total average return is 785%* — a market-crushing outperformance compared to 200% for the S&P 500.

They just revealed what they believe are the 10 best stocks for investors to buy right now, available when you join Stock Advisor.

Netflix's The Body was one of the unexpected hits of the 2026 TV schedule. While it's still yet to be confirmed whether we'll get to stream a second season with a Netflix subscription, it feels like the odds are looking good. So I'm glad the executive producers/showrunners are already shooting down some fan theories before any new episodes come out.

Unfortunately, creator Quinn Shephard and co-showrunner Gina Gammell didn't tell Glamour all the biggest secrets surrounding Grace and whether this is truly spiritual or classic Catholic School drama, Luckily, though, fans did learn a couple of key details. I'm happy about both of these theories being addressed, especially the one that sounds like good news for Leo.

If you're a fan of Leo and think he's too pure for this world, Quinn Shepard confirming he did not bring a snake to the Halloween party should bring you some comfort. It's been theorized that Leo brought his pet snake to the party, perhaps in an attempt to heal Grace. This was supported by the fact that Leo and Grace came from a cult called Signs Following, which used snake handling as a means of showing the faith of its followers.

Additionally, Leo seemed concerned about other characters outside of his family seeing the snake during the investigation into Grace's coma. With this context, it's possible that Leo was just concerned about hiding their true religious beliefs, as they lied about being Catholics to get rehomed after leaving West Virginia.

I'm so glad the EPs have also confirmed the Lions won't be going to jail after what happened to Grace. I thought that was a possibility, given Anya's confession and the various details about the party that came out toward the end of Season 1.

I Just Watched The Trailer For Netflix's JonBenèt Ramsey Drama, And I Feel A Bit Uneasy

While I'm all for accountability in teen dramas, especially when it comes to Riverdale's Archie Andrews, I'll let it slide for The Body. Sending Anya or any of the Lions to jail or prison for their actions at the party would've created a major genre shift in the story, and I'm far more interested in the story remaining around the school after Season 1's ending.

Of course, we still have to wait for official word on Season 2 of The Body. While I think Netflix should be bullish on a renewal given the talented young cast and the fact it's been on Netflix's Top 10 charts in recent weeks, there's really no telling what the streamer will decide to do with the series. It's a well known fact that the company has canceled plenty of shows way too soon, so we best just cross our fingers and hope a Grace-sized miracle grants the series a renewal soon.

As mentioned, The Body is available to stream, and there are other upcoming Netflix shows that will be worth checking out as well. With the holidays approaching, now is the time to catch up on everything you missed this year, and there's so much to enjoy!

Sens. Warren, Van Hollen lead push to scrap SEC’s Regulation Crypto and innovation exemption

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

In a letter sent to SEC Chair Atkins, Sens. Warren and Van Hollen said the agency's "Regulation Crypto Assets" would allow crypto firms to circumvent securities laws.

Top Democrats Sen. Elizabeth Warren and Chris Van Hollen are demanding that Securities and Exchange Commission Chair Paul Atkins take down its crypto rulemaking governing how crypto projects can raise capital and how tokenized securities can be traded.

In two separate letters sent to Atkins on Tuesday, Sens. Warren, Van Hollen and Tina Smith and other Senate Banking Committee members said the agency's "Regulation Crypto Assets" would allow crypto firms to circumvent securities laws, weaken state jurisdiction and could harm investors.

"The proposal would create a new 'tailored securities offering regime' for certain investment contracts involving crypto assets," the senators wrote. "However, the proposal only serves to help crypto assets escape from the SEC's regulatory regime and gives them special treatment—with less oversight—compared to other traditional securities."

In August, the SEC proposed crypto offering rules to offer startup and fundraising exemptions for digital asset offerings. It allows for a "startup exemption" that would exempt offerings of up to $5 million from registration rules under the Securities Act of 1933 for four years — a federal law focused on anti-fraud and requiring certain disclosures. The proposal also includes a "fundraising exemption" for offerings up to $75 million for a year.

Warren is poised to take on a more prominent role following Democrats’ victories in November's midterm elections, which handed the party control of both chambers of Congress. Warren has been critical of crypto in the past and has particularly brought up concerns around illicit finance and money laundering. Meanwhile, Van Hollen could be the next Senate Majority Leader. He has since challenged current Senate Minority Leader Chuck Schumer for that role.

Sens. Jack Reed and Tina Smith join the two in the second letter asking the SEC to withdraw its innovation exemption — a measure released in September to bring capital markets onchain through the trading of tokenized stocks. It is a five-year exemption that would exempt certain trading venues, called tokenized securities venues, from being defined as an exchange and also exempts specific liquidity providers from being defined as a dealer under securities law when they trade stocks or provide liquidity through automated market makers.

The senators said it would allow platforms to operate outside of certain safeguards, including involving fair pricing and market transparency.

"The order would allow TSV platforms to operate a shadow stock market outside longstanding regulatory protections designed to ensure fair and transparent markets, impose new burdens on publicly traded companies seeking to protect their shareholders, and create unnecessary risks of illicit financial activity," the senators wrote.

The three senators also said the innovation exemption could cause capital markets to splinter.

"As concerned stakeholders have pointed out, fragmenting our securities markets by creating a lightly regulated alternative marketplace for tokenized securities unnecessarily risks dividing liquidity into isolated pools and expanding opportunities for corrosive regulatory arbitrage," they said.

A separate group of Senate Democrats have also brought up concerns about the innovation exemption. Last week, Sens. Angela Alsobrooks, Ruben Gallego, Mark Warner and Catherine Cortez Masto said it could open the door for illicit finance actors and also had concerns about protections for retail investors around pricing.

The SEC did not immediately respond to a request for comment.

Thirty-one killed in massive fire as fuel tanker crashes on Cairo highway

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Huge fire erupts near Cairo killing at least 30 people

Thirty-one people have been killed and 21 others injured in a large fire on a busy highway in Cairo after a fuel tanker crashed, Egypt's health ministry says.

Hospitals nearby treated patients suffering burns and smoke inhalation after the incident, which occurred on a section of a ring road in the Warraq neighbourhood, to the north of the old city centre, on Wednesday evening.

Unverified footage circulating on social media showed billowing flames and black smoke towering high into the night sky, with multiple nearby vehicles torched.

Officials have not given an account of how the fire broke out, but state-owned media reported the tanker had overturned, dousing nearby vehicles with petrol.

The regional public prosecutor has launched an investigation into the incident and asked that DNA samples be taken from bodies of the dead.

The tanker, which belonged to a private contractor, had been travelling along the highway when a high-voltage cable fell in front of it, prompting the driver to lose control, Al-Ahram reports, citing sources with knowledge of the investigation.

It then crossed the central reservation, overturned and caught fire, the state-owned newspaper says.

Egypt has suffered several deadly fires in recent years, with informal residential areas and often run-down infrastructure making incidents more common, the response to which is often complicated by slow emergency response times and locally improvised rescue efforts, even in the sprawling Egyptian capital.

Images from the scene show a large crowd gathered on the highway after the fire had been extinguished, as emergency services blocked off entry routes.

Images from the scene show a large crowd gathered on the highway after the fire had been extinguished

The tanker crossed the highway's central reservation before overturning and bursting into flames, local media report

The health ministry said 39 ambulances had been dispatched to respond to the fire, while it posted images of Egypt's health minister overseeing the treatment of the injured at one of Cairo's hospitals.

Road collisions are also common in Egypt, often attributed to lax maintenance and the widespread flouting of traffic laws.

Nearly 6,000 people died on Egypt's roads last year, official figures show, a rise of several hundred on the previous year's total.

Emergency responders have since cleared the highway and allowed it to reopen, state broadcaster NMA reports.

A bulldozer could earlier be seen at the scene clearing the burnt-out wrecks of cars.

Study: Meta settlement needs YouTube, TikTok to succeed

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

When Meta settled a massive social media addiction lawsuit brought by multiple state attorneys general in August, it invited YouTube and TikTok to join the terms.

Meta, which agreed to implement new restrictions on teen use, argued that the safeguards amounted to a new industry standard. They included a two-hour daily time limit, removing nighttime access to Meta apps by default, and no notifications during school hours.

"These protections will only be truly effective if we work with our peers — TikTok and YouTube — to put the same measures in place," Meta said in a statement at the time. Neither social media platform has joined the settlement since.

Now, a new study published in JAMA suggests Meta was right about the potential effect of YouTube and TikTok agreeing to the restrictions.

The authors analyzed existing data from ongoing research known as the Adolescent Brain Cognitive Development (ABCD) Study, which tracks teen screen time and social media habits. They looked at data from 1,803 participants over a five-year period between Nov. 2020 and Aug. 2025.

They found the Meta apps Instagram and Facebook accounted for only 20 percent of total daily social media use amongst those participants. Snapchat, TikTok, and YouTube comprised the remaining apps named in the litigation.

Additionally, the authors found that significantly more teens would benefit from the time limit, nighttime access, and notification restrictions if the other companies implemented them.

"I think many people hailed that settlement with a huge sigh of relief, but unless the other companies join suit, these data suggest it's not going to make a huge difference," Dr. Dimitri Christakis, principal investigator at the Center for Child Health, Behavior and Development at Seattle Children's, said in a statement. (Christakis has served as an expert witness in social media litigation and has received consulting fees from the attorneys general of California, New Mexico, Virginia, and Washington.) The best tech deal this week The Apple iPhone 18 Pro is free right now with T-Mobile Products available for purchase through affiliate links. If you buy something through links on our site, Mashable may earn an affiliate commission.

The Apple iPhone 18 Pro is free right now with T-Mobile

Dr. Jason Nagata, an associate professor of pediatrics at the University of California, San Francisco who was not involved in the study, said the real-world phone-use data allowed the authors to examine how the settlement's proposed protections might come into play.

The daily time limit, for example, would affect just 7.7 percent of teens in the ABCD study. If implemented by TikTok, YouTube, and Snapchat, that would rise to 63 percent.

Blocking Meta access from midnight to 6 a.m. would affect more than three-quarters of Meta users. By contrast, the same rule applied to TikTok, YouTube, and Snapchat would cover nearly 90 percent of users in the ABCD study.

There's a similar disparity for overnight notifications. Shutting them off between 10 p.m. and 7 a.m. would affect almost two-thirds of Meta users, but 86 percent across TikTok, YouTube, Snapchat, Facebook, and Instagram.

"The study shows how difficult it can be to address social media use one company at a time when teens regularly move across many different platforms," said Nagata, who previously used ABCD data to study smartphone use at night and during school.

Nagata noted that the JAMA study doesn't indicate how well the proposed restrictions will work in the real world. Teens might change their behavior, shift to other platforms, or develop workarounds. Nagata believes future research must evaluate which restrictions reduce problematic use and improve teen health and well-being.

The authors acknowledged other important limitations in their research. The ABCD study sample included only Android users, not iPhone users, so it may not reflect the experiences of all U.S. teens. The passive phone tracking may miss use on other devices like tablets or laptops. The study didn't capture the content viewed by users, which may affect teens differently. The estimated reach of the restrictions is also hypothetical.

Still, the researchers argue their estimates provide a baseline for understanding the restrictions' potential impact.

"The settlement will affect some teenagers but not enough,” Christakis said.
