Einsia AI Open-Source RoboUniverse-10K, the First 10K-Scale RL Environment Suite for Agential Robotics

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The open-source project includes 10,807 physics-interactive simulation tasks and a 500-task benchmark; fine-tuning improves task success from 0.8% to 21.2%.

SINGAPORE, Nov. 25, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Einsia AI today announced the release of RoboUniverse-10K, an open-source suite of 10,807 interactive physics simulation environments designed for embodied AI agent training. Developed in collaboration with researchers from Tsinghua University, Peking University, the University of Illinois Urbana-Champaign (UIUC), the University of Hong Kong (HKU), University College London (UCL), and the University of North Carolina at Chapel Hill (UNC-Chapel Hill), the project aims to address a fundamental bottleneck in embodied AI: the lack of scalable, physically realistic, and verifiable training environments.

The release also includes RoboUniverse-Verified, an independent benchmark comprising 500 held-out tasks. Using approximately 17,000 multi-turn interaction trajectories collected from the environments, the team fine-tuned a Qwen3.6-35B-A3B-based model, improving its task success rate from 0.8% to 21.2%. Experiments across additional simulation benchmarks and on a real Franka robot further demonstrate the potential of the approach.

Large language models have benefited from scaling training data across the internet, while coding agents have advanced through executable environments built from real-world software repositories, such as SWE-Gym and SWE-smith. Embodied AI faces a more complex challenge: agents must interact with physical environments governed by realistic dynamics, while relying on accurate feedback to determine whether their actions have succeeded.

Yet simulation environments have traditionally been expensive and labor-intensive to build, limiting the number and diversity of available tasks. Without reliable physics and task verification, agents may also exploit simulation flaws, such as passing through objects or using unrealistic movements to obtain rewards without actually completing a task.

RoboUniverse-10K addresses these challenges by turning real-world robot manipulation videos into executable and verifiable simulation environments.

Turning Real-World Robot Data into 10,807 Interactive Environments

The project covers both single-arm and dual-arm manipulation tasks. Its single-arm environments draw primarily from DROID, alongside more than ten datasets including BridgeData V2, BC-Z, RT-1, and RoboCook. Dual-arm tasks incorporate data from RoboDesk, RoboCOIN, GM100, and RoboMIND2, among others.

To ensure that the dataset reflects genuine task diversity rather than superficial variations in object appearance or placement, the team established a five-level deduplication framework covering robot configuration, task family, individual task, scene layout, and manipulation trajectory.

The resulting dataset contains 10,807 distinct tasks, including 10,307 training tasks and 500 held-out evaluation tasks. Its scale is approximately 30 times that of RoboCasa365, with dual-arm tasks accounting for more than 60% of the collection.

At the core of the pipeline is an automated Real-to-Sim workflow powered by Coding Agents. Using tools such as SAM 3.1 for segmentation and DA3 for depth estimation, the system extracts task-relevant information from real-world videos. Coding Agents then construct simulation scenes, reference solutions, and task verifiers in Isaac Sim, iteratively refining the environments through comparisons between real footage and simulated execution.

An independent QA Agent reviews the source videos, simulation replays, and state logs to assess physical fidelity, reset reproducibility, and verifier robustness. Environments containing issues such as object penetration, teleportation, or false-positive success detection are rejected before entering the dataset.

Evaluating Agents Without Privileged Information

RoboUniverse-10K also introduces a strict execution-and-verification architecture compatible with Harbor, isolating the agent's execution environment from the simulation backend and task verifiers.

Agents can interact with the environment only through five public interfaces. They cannot access hidden object coordinates, reference solutions, or verification logic. Simulation time advances only when actions are executed, not while the agent is reasoning or analyzing visual observations. The environment provides no intermediate progress rewards and does not allow agents to reset tasks themselves. Each task permits only one attempt, with success determined independently by the verifier based on the final physical state.

These constraints are designed to ensure that agents complete tasks through visual perception and action control, rather than relying on privileged information or shortcuts unavailable in the real world.

Benchmark Results Reveal Key Gaps in Embodied Agent Capabilities

The team evaluated 11 leading closed-source and open-source models on RoboUniverse-Verified. GPT-6.2 Astra achieved the highest success rate at 85.8%, followed by Claude Opus 5.5 at 76.8%. GPT-6 Astra, GPT-6.1 Sol scored approximately 50%, while the remaining seven models each achieved no more than 10%. The strongest native open-source baseline reached 10.0%.

Further analysis identified three major capability gaps.

First, grasping remains a fundamental challenge. Models with overall success rates below 10% achieved an average success rate of 32.1% on non-grasping tasks, such as pushing and pressing, but only 2.7% on grasping tasks. By comparison, the four leading models achieved an average of 63.2% on grasping tasks.

Second, flexible-object manipulation remains difficult even for frontier models. While leading models improved by 35â38 percentage points on rigid-object tasks, their performance on cloth manipulation improved by only around three percentage points, with success rates remaining between 28% and 41%.

Third, precise control after contact is a major bottleneck. Approximately 80% of failures among leading models occurred after initial contact with an object. In one cup-lid assembly task, failed attempts showed an average offset of 6 mm and a tilt of 5.6Â°, exceeding the tolerances of 2 mm and 4Â°. In another task, some models applied excessive force to remote-control buttons, reaching 70â138 N.

These findings suggest that the next stage of embodied agent development will require more than improved visual recognition and task planning. Reliable grasping, contact-aware control, and the ability to respond to continuous physical feedback remain critical challenges.

Fine-Tuning Improves Task Success from 0.8% to 21.2%

To test whether RoboUniverse-10K can translate into measurable capability improvements, the team conducted a post-training experiment using Qwen3.6-35B-A3B as the base model.

The researchers freezing the visual encoder and alignment modules while fully fine-tuning the language backbone. The resulting model, RoboUniverse-35B-A3B, achieved a task success rate of 21.2% on RoboUniverse-Verified, up from 0.8% for the base model.

Performance on single-arm tasks increased from 2.0% to 40.5%, while success on pick-and-place tasks rose from 0% to 62.2%. The model also achieved an 8.3% success rate on dual-arm tasks, where the base model had previously recorded no successful completions.

The fine-tuned model also completed tasks in three additional simulation benchmarks: LIBERO-MAX, RoboCasa, and RoboTwin 2.0 where the base model had failed to complete the corresponding tasks.

In a real-world experiment, the model was deployed on a Franka robotic arm and successfully completed a whiteboard drawing task not included in its training data. Given a reference image, the robot drew the Einsia logo while adapting to an unknown whiteboard height through visual observation, trial movements, and continuous correction.

Building Scalable Infrastructure for Embodied AI

RoboUniverse-10K, including its environments, benchmark, and related fine-tuned model, is now open source. The release is intended to provide researchers with a reusable foundation for embodied agent training, evaluation, and further research.

While the current post-training results are based on supervised fine-tuning rather than reinforcement learning, the project demonstrates the potential value of scalable, physically grounded environments for improving embodied agent capabilities.

Just as large-scale internet text helped advance language models and real-world code repositories provided training grounds for coding agents, RoboUniverse-10K aims to establish scalable physical-world training infrastructure for embodied AI, helping the field move toward an era of environment scaling.

Founded in 2026, Einsia AI is a research-driven AI lab focused on advancing the frontiers of artificial intelligence. Through the development of authoritative model benchmarks and research into advanced AI training methods, Einsia AI aims to expand the boundaries of intelligence aligned with the real world.

SOURCE Einsia AI Technology (SG) PTE. LTD.

Kimes joined Torre and The Athletic's Charles McDonald to taste Reid's infamous mac-and-cheese that was being cooked by celebrity chef David Chang. While the reporters were enjoying the food, which was "so f*cking good," Kimes couldn't help but to get a jab in at Butker's expense.

During his speech, he said women are likely more excited about getting married and having children than getting their college degrees.

Like always, Kimes knows how to land a perfect callback joke, and she managed to outshine Andy Reid's mac-and-cheese with her latest one-liner.

Did Kent State get completely robbed by controversial replay review?

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The great thing about sports is that there can never be too much sports. Even on a feast-filled week of college basketball, college football and elite NFL action, there is always room on the television for some MAC-tion involving lower-level teams. So you can bet that Kent State-Eastern Michigan was proudly playing as families all over this country reconnected for the beginning of Thanksgiving activities on Tuesday night. And those who managed to stick around to the end were treated to a thoroughly entertaining ending.

It didn't make sense, but who could argue that it didn't provide all sorts of drama.

MORE: College football rankings 2026: CFP poll, updated bracket for Rivalry Weekend

With the score 17-12 in Eastern Michigan's favor, Kent State faced a 4th-and-9 from the +15-yard line. Six reaming seconds on the clock signaled that this would probably serve as the game's final play.

Kent State's Wayne Harris appeared to have hauled in a contest-deciding touchdown grab in the back of the end zone. Then MAC replay officials intervened to make things much more confusing.

Just watch this play. Yes, it's been decades of not knowing as a society what actually constitutes a catch. This still looks and feels like a catch. Replay officials, however, felt differently and overturned the call to give Eastern Michigan a victory and leave the Golden Flashes stunned.

Just when you think that you, a casual football fan, gets the catch rule—something like this happens. A few days after the Parker Washington catch that wasn't actually a catch. It's alright if you feel confused.

MORE: Tee Higgins' controversial TD proves catch definition still eludes NFL referees

On the Kent State one, a deep dive into the comments suggests that an end zone angle shows that Harris did not establish complete control of the ball until his first foot down came off the ground. Then it becomes tough to tell which part of his body hits the ground first as he falls backward. That feels like something a person would have to try very, very hard to see. To be quite candid I couldn't find this mythical freeze frame on the internet.

It's a very dumb guy take but there is something to the idea that these calls would be better handled by a slightly-altered dude eating chips on his couch. Like, 99.9 percent of that subset would watch this once and say "heck yeah, that's a touchdown, dude."

Would that be worse than this? Would it be worse than everyone in the world thinking something is a catch only to be told it's not? Tough to say.

Tired of GTA 6? Here's 23 lesser-known gems that might beat it at its own game

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

You can do a lot of things in GTA 6: fishing, golfing, running, jumping, dancing, racing, roaming, shopping. It goes on! There's romancing, birdwatching, DIY, and even more besides. It really is an everything game. But GTA 6 isn't for everyone.

So what if you were in the mood for some of this stuff but away from Leonida? Where else might you go? Consider this the slightly tenuous set-up, as we continue a week of Not-GTA, for us to highlight some of our favourite smaller games; brilliant lesser-known titles from the last 12 months or so that, in their own special way, beat GTA 6 at its own game.

It's very sweet that the central, driving force of GTA 6 is the strained romance between Jason and Lucia. You are, essentially, playing a virtual matchmaker, helping these star-crossed lovers reconnect and rediscover each other after fate conspires to drive a wedge between them. But you know what's better than that? Being an actual matchmaker, matching icons on a grid and enjoying the microdose dopamine rush when you clear a line on a board.

So here's Titanium Court, which goes even further: with every line cleared, you come closer to escaping a weird, 80s-core fairy castle in a game that's partly telling a story about introspective self-doubt, and partly about warring fairies. If you're getting tired of texting your virtual lover every 10 minutes, perhaps try lighting a virtual cigarette in Titanium Court, instead - I've heard it does wonders for your health.

How do you find out if you can really depend upon someone? That's a question at the heart of GTA 6. And the answer is: play Big Walk. There's nothing like gesturing through the window of a sound-proofed room hoping your friend or loved one will understand what two arms waggling around means. It means 'the high-pitched warbling sound plaque', obviously. Big Walk's wonderfully stripped-back world is also an antidote to GTA 6's maximal world. The silence and space in the game is absolutely the point. What will you fill it with?

It's not all roof repairs and late night paintjobs. Sometimes home improvement requires more drastic measures. And in Virtue and a Sledgehammer, that's taken to violent extremes. This is a game, in developers Selkie Harbour and Deconstructeam's words, about family, tradition, and being a misfit. Where an entire town - the protagonist's childhood home - faces a total spiritual teardown. Here, a brutal sledgehammer-swinging reckoning awaits as you unearth memories - some happy, some decidedly not - through violent destruction, cutting through mechanical human facsimiles with the faces of the ones you love. It's haunting, devastating stuff; a very different kind of do-it-yourself.

You work, you sleep, and then you die. That's how it's got to be. So if you're going to toil mercilessly for the man, you can at least hope for an interesting employee. And what's more interesting than the weirdo threesome at the centre of Servant of the Lake?

Developer Rusty Lake's eponymous series returned this year with another macabre blend of point-and-click puzzling and room-escape-style adventure. And it's a corker. Perhaps not as dazzlingly experimental as some other recent entries, but it's still a delightfully grim time as you work through your three days of servitude, butlering for the oddball Vanderboom family. Beware the blood moon.

You work, you sleep, and then you die. But! What if you dream of breaking free? Escaping the chains of drudgery? That's the starting point of Denshattack!, a Jet Set Radio-inspired adventure all about sticking it to the man. Or, more specifically, a shady megacorp trying to suppress human ingenuity, creativity, and spirit. It is, as Dom put it in their five-star review, a game of "anti-capitalist, pop-punk optimism", which sees you careening across a colourful Japan - as a speeding, trick-flipping train, of all things - to bring the Miraidō corporation to its knees. Stylish and satisfying.

Umph-umph-umph-umph umph-umph-umph-umph. Oh yeah, that's the good stuff. If you want to shoot things and see them explode, and do that with immense satisfaction and a hypnotic, pulsating electronic soundtrack, this is the one. Sektori takes the feedback of a satisfying trigger pull and boils that down to its purest essence, often in spiralling, kaleidoscopic form. Hitting a full set of letters - M-I-R-A-G-E, S-E-K-T-O-R-I, etc. - summons a rainbow of action that feels like genuine ecstasy. Pure blasting and survival.

Umph-umph-umph-umph umph-umph-umph-umph. But this time imagine it's a disco beat. Let's breathe in the night, bask in those pulsing lights, and pretend we'll live this moment forever. Or, you know, we'll just shuffle our way through the throng of the dancefloor like a massive game of sokoban. That latter's the premise of developer Miracle Tea's Club Soko; a game about traversing an underground nightclub - one shunt at a time - to discover the secrets it holds. And it's good 'un, bringing a thinkier rhythm to its pounding beat.

Most people think of fishing as a calm escape from the hustle and bustle of daily life - that's certainly the case of GTA 6. But relaxation, I say, is for posers, which brings us to How to Fish. In developer Dazed Games' silly, chaotic physics-based fishing sim, you're given an arsenal of guns to unload upon fish and foe alike. Beware the seagulls, who have learned to love the taste of human flesh over the bounty of humble chip shops. It's great fun, especially co-operatively, so grab some pals and have a blast.

Neasied, the island where Queen's Domain unfolds, might not be as dizzyingly expansive as some open-worlds, but the stuff it manages to pack into a relatively compact space is astonishing. This is a game crammed with secrets, rich with mystery; a world so dense with enigmas it's a joy to explore. There's more than a touch of FromSoftware to its broader retro-inspired combat rhythms, but there are hints of Zelda and Metroidvania too. It's one of the best games this year - full of surprise and wonder as its absurdly elaborate puzzle threads unfurl - and an extraordinary adventure.

Money too tight to mention? How about ditching the life of crime for a spot of restaurant entrepreneurialism? That's the idea behind Nivalis Night - and, actually, the life of crime is still an option. First seen in 2020 courier adventure Cloudpunk, Nivalis is a striking, sprawling cyberpunk dystopia of flying cars, neon lights, and suffocating smog that sets itself apart with its lo-fi style. And in this year's follow-up, you can finally get out of your car and live in the world. But it's not just a nifty first-person tycoon sim, it's a game of stories, and developer Ion Lands imbues its city with real warmth and humanity; a perfect accompaniment, it turns out, to all the Vangelis-like synths and sushi.

There's street racing and then there's developer Milestone's Screamer. It's a narrative-focused racing game which leans into a far arcadier sort of drive compared to the more realistic Grand Theft Auto. It's also got a great anime aesthetic, so you can live out any MF Ghost fantasies you have tucked away. Or maybe Initial D for the drifting aficionados among you.

Okay, fine, so it's more of a slap than a punch, but developer Double Fine's Thank You Bus Driver still packs in more violence than you'd expect from a driving sim. This game jam experiment turned full release feels a bit like a first-person Crazy Taxi as you try and get passengers to their destination before time runs out. Happy passengers means more 'thank yous', which means more time for more pick-ups, and it's a brilliantly chaotic loop.

It's got bags of personality - its scrappy black-and-white doodle town and oddball passengers are a treat - and a bunch of neat hooks. Most notably, HQ intermittently imposes new rules - no talking! No toenail clipping! - which is where slaps come in. Rulebreakers need discipline if you don't want to lose your 'thank yous', so you spin around and wallop them about the chops. Just try not to drive off the road.

Sure, Leonida's landscape holds its own kind of sunkissed beauty, but The Game Bakers has created something really special with Cairn. It might be a game focused on mountain climbing, but it's hard not to stop and admire the view whenever you reach the next plateau. As you make your way up Mount Kami, you're greeted with almost constant lovely views, some of the most gorgeous vistas released this year. Cairn is the sort of game that warrants putting the controller down and just taking the sights in for a moment or two.

As a doctor of dubious repute once said, where we're going, we don't need roads! So wave goodbye to metropolitan squalor, farewell to suburban suffocation, and hello the great outdoors. Over the Hill, the follow-up to Funselektor's brilliant Art of Rally, is all about off-road roaming through the vast, untamed wilderness. It's a game of purposeful meandering and patient delights and you explore the secrets of, among other places, its starlit deserts and rolling forest hills. You'll bounce your 4x4 over rocks, crawl sluggishly up hills, and winch through squelching mud, and it's all so slow. That's deliberate though. And as the result, amid the gentle strum of soothing guitar, is a wonderfully meditative game.

There are few other games released this year that nail the ruthlessness of vehicle-to-vehicle combat like Airframe Ultra. An absolutely gorgeous battle-racer in which you take control of a bulky airframe. You can smack the daylights out of other riders with chains, bats, and other makeshift melee weapons. Failing that, ramming into people at full speed works wonders too. A gritty game that pleases the eye and nourishes the violent soul.

Life, eh? One minute it's all Leonida sunshine and motorboating, the next you're on the run. But it's how you deal with each roll of the dice that matters. And that's very much the premise of Cosmo D's wonderfully weird Moves of the Diamond Hand. It's back to the jazz-drenched, oddball world of Off-Peak City we go, this time as election day looms. The legendary Circus X has returned to town and you want a place in its show. What follows is a first-person narrative adventure that takes the tabletop RPG-inspired dice-throwing of Cosmo D's acclaimed 2022 game Betrayal at Club Low and turns it into something richer, deeper, wilder, and weirder. And yes, it's got a killer soundtrack.

I see you smirking, pal - cut it out. Early on in GTA 6 you're tasked with hosing off a boat in the marina. And if that scratches an unknown itch within you, PowerWash Simulator 2 has all the nozzle blasting you could hope for. Yes, it's a slightly older one, but all 2026's DLC means it still feels new. Even more grimy buildings and filthy objects are waiting for a hero with a soap dispenser, just begging for a deep clean. Is that you? Then live your dream!

Browsing the tourist traps and fashion emporiums for the next big look is all well and good, but have you ever considered taking matters into your own hands? Dressmaking might not seem like the most appealing way to spend your time if you're not particularly into wearing dresses or seamstressing. (It's me, I'm that person.) However, Dressmaker and the sheer amount of clothing combinations it makes possible are a very pleasant surprise.

Weaved throughout it all is a story that sees you designing dresses for different characters and the events they're attending, then stitching your ideas to life piece by piece with tactile minigames, And you can design your own dresses too! I can only imagine GTA 6's Jason might even enjoy designing a dress or two for Lucia if he had Dressmaker on his JoyStation...

Creator, writer, director, composer, and 'perceptual architect' Johannes Zuch's Tenebrae Lux might be wowing the critics in GTA 6, but for my money this year's best game-within-a-game is Forbidden Solitaire. It's 2019 and you've stumbled upon an old copy of notorious CD-ROM adventure Forbidden Solitaire. It was pulled from the shelves amid some mysterious deaths, but that won't stop you from playing. What follows is a wonderfully creepy haunted media yarn - told across police records, press clippings, 90s local news reports, grisly photos, even a recreation of a cheesy true-crime-style mystery show - which brilliantly evokes the 90s multimedia boom with its lurid, awkward pre-rendered game-in-a-game visuals. It also, in case you're wondering, plays a damn good game of solitaire.

I like birds. I like them so much that it's at the top of my bucket list to find a Wonga Pigeon one day. So, you can imagine how I felt finding out that GTA 6 has over 60 birds to spot, alongside binoculars and a phone camera to fulfil my bird-watching dreams.

Before GTA 6 however, there came a lovely little multiplayer game called Flock Around developed by Friend Shaped Games that lets you explore multiple biomes while trying to snap photographs of birds from around the world and fill out your guide book. I was enamoured by its simple loop of wandering around and waiting for birds to strike a perfect pose, take selfies with them, and maybe even watching them empty their insides all over your friends. I don't think it's possible to be pooped on by a bird in GTA 6, so by default, Flock Around takes the crown as one of the best games for snapping pics of birds this year.

GTA 6 might use the gorgeous relationship between Lucia and Jason to demonstrate what basic human empathy looks like in a culture that promotes instant gratification and selfishness, but Penguin Colony uses a, erm, penguin colony to highlight the existential dangers of fascism and the pervasive threat of racism and colonialism. I know which piece of social commentary I prefer.

It's not hard to have a better cleaning regimen than GTA 6's Jason: the man apparently isn't aware his nethers need a wash too. But designer Robert Yang's Rinse and Repeat's has a solution for those struggling at showertime: get someone else to help you. Rinse and Repeat is a "steamy first-person showering game ... about consent and safety" and got a remaster earlier this year as part of Yang's Radiator Forever compilation. Yang's award-winning work frequently explores queer subculture, and the NSFW compilation also contains three other games - Hurt Me Plenty, Succulent, and Stick Shift - with more to follow. Better yet, it's completely free.

Forget cracking safes in GTA 6, what about cracking the codes in a machine that seems to be, well, more than it seems? This is the hook in Inkle's TR-49, a game about a machine that once lurked in the mysterious depths of Bletchley Park's legendary codebreaking vaults, alongside the Enigma Machine. This machine works differently. It's effectively an archive you search around using codes in the format of letter-letter, number-number, hence TR-49. But the codes you use and what you find, and how you find them: that's up to you. And sure, there's probably not gold bullion on the other side of this code, but perhaps there's something even more rewarding.

Morning Minute: Ethereum Focuses on Security As AI Threats Rise

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Morning Minute: Ethereum Focuses on Security As AI Threats Rise

Morning Minute is a daily newsletter written by Tyler Warner. The analysis and opinions expressed are his own and do not necessarily reflect those of Decrypt. And check out our daily news show 'FOMO HOUR' covering all of the top stories and market action.

The Ethereum Foundation has doubled its protocol security team and made cryptography its top priority. The reason isn’t quantum computers.

Protocol lead Kev Wedderburn wrote this week that “recently AI emerged as a more urgent risk”, and the distinction matters. The quantum threat has always been a hardware problem, and hardware problems announce themselves years in advance because somebody has to build the machine. The AI version is a math problem. A sufficiently capable model could find a structural shortcut in the elliptic curve cryptography that secures Ethereum and Bitcoin, and it would need no special hardware to do it.

Researcher Justin Drake raised the scenario in October. In a worst case, he said, existing signatures could become vulnerable within months.

An Anthropic mathematician named Levent Alpöge posted a claimed proof of the Riemann hypothesis, produced with an unreleased version of Claude, along with a formalized proof archive. Nobody has verified it. It doesn’t break elliptic curve cryptography and nobody is claiming it does. What it does is demonstrate the capability class Drake was describing, roughly a month after he described it.

Axios reported Tuesday that more than two dozen people familiar with internal discussions at OpenAI and Anthropic say employees worry their most advanced unreleased models may never be safe enough to release. The concerns named include reward hacking, models that hide or misrepresent their reasoning, and systems capable enough to find weaknesses in the safety measures their own developers built. OpenAI’s chief scientist proposed Sunday that labs agree on shared limits on how far they train models.

You can now act on the news you read: Go long or short ETH with perpetual futures, all from your Decrypt Money Account.

The plan replaces elliptic curves with hash-based cryptography, which rests on fewer structural assumptions about mathematics. It touches how users authorize transactions, how validators secure the network, and how layer-2 data gets verified. Drake proposed pulling the deadline from December 2029 to December 2028. Ethlabs withdrew its faster-block-production proposal from the Hegotá upgrade to free up engineers. The Foundation has deployed AI agents to hunt bugs in its own infrastructure, which is a reasonable response and also an admission about where the advantage currently sits.

Wedderburn’s own framing: it’s harder than the Merge, and there’s less time. He also acknowledged that moving faster makes it easier to ship bugs.

Bitcoin is on the same road. Blockstream’s Jonas Nick published a security analysis of SHRINCS last week, the apparent frontrunner to replace Bitcoin’s signatures, with sizes running from 548 bytes to 5,777 against today’s 64 to 71.

So the AI threat is looming (and accelerating), but the two largest crypto assets have teams that are responding. Now we wait on action, and more assurances that crypto is safe from AI threats. Assurances that could ensure the bottom is in fact in, if and when they arrive…

ESSENCE Festival of Culture Moving On From New Orleans After Three Decades: ‘Memories Were Made — And So Was History’

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The beloved event first took place in the Big Easy in 1995 with a lineup that included Aaliyah, Aretha Franklin, Mary J. Blige, Luther Vandross and more.

After three decades of bringing R&B, funk, hip-hop, soul, blues and zydeco to generations of fans, the ESSENCE Festival of Culture announced on Tuesday (Nov. 24) that it is moving on from New Orleans. In an open letter celebrating 31 years in the Big Easy, Essence thanked the city it has called home since launching in 1995. Explore Billboard See latest videos, charts and news

“For more than three decades, you’ve been more than the home of the ESSENCE Festival of Culture. Through every year and every chapter of our annual event, you’ve been part of our family: a destination and community instrumental to our journey, and a culture that has informed and inspired us with every gorgeous sound, unforgettable flavor, and vibrant color,” read the letter. Related Ella Langley's 'Choosin' Texas' Ties Hot Country Songs Record With 50 Weeks at No. 1 Post Malone, Travis Scott & Charli xcx to Headline Tecate Pa'l Norte 2027: See the Full Lineup Pearl Jam Slams 'Cold Cruelty' of ICE After 'Unconscionable' Detainments at Seattle Airport

“Your hospitality has welcomed us. Your traditions have taught us. Every summer, your spirit lifted us,” it continued. “From the sounds of Frankie Beverly & Maze echoing through the Caesars Superdome and the generations who came together in the Ernest N. Morial Convention Center, to the laughter filling the streets, the family-owned restaurants that fed us, and the warm drawl of a ‘bayyy-by,’ every July, you gave our celebration a rhythm that could only belong to New Orleans.”

The farewell noted that the annual gathering had grown into more than just a weekend of music, morphing over the years into a “reunion, a tradition, and a celebration that could only have been shaped by a city like yours. It was a homecoming … It was also a chance to connect with the community: the local artists who shared their gifts, the small business owners who opened their doors, the hospitality workers who made us feel at home, and the culture bearers, community leaders, and everyday New Orleanians who shared their city, their traditions and their hearts with us. We are forever grateful for your partnership. Thank you for helping us tell our stories, spark conversations, empower Black creators from all over the country at the We Love Us Market, and fill the Superdome with joyful spectacle year after year.”

ESSENCE Festival launched in 1995 as a celebration of the 25th anniversary of its namesake magazine with the event’s patented mix of old-school soul, blues, R&B and pop featuring Aaliyah, Aretha Franklin, B.B. King, Gladys Knight, Luther Vandross and Mary J. Blige, as well as appearances by speakers including Dr. Cornel West, Jesse Jackson and Johnny Cochran.

WDSU posted a statement from festival organizers the Sundial Media Group explaining that the decision to pull up stakes on the event that began as a concert and spread into the “largest celebration of Black culture in the world” featuring film, book, food and wine events and free community programming did not come easily. It noted that in 2025, the festival generated more than $300 million in economic impact for New Orleans and Louisiana, but that after several months seeking a multi-year commitment and a reported lack of engagement from city officials, talks appear to have broken down.

“The city’s RFQ (request for quote) this summer signaled it never intended to offer ESSENCE a multiyear agreement. To be clear, talks have not stalled over vendor payments. As shared, we are resolving all outstanding payments to New Orleans and Louisiana vendors for the 2026 Festival, and every vendor will be paid and reconciled,” read the statement from Sundial. “The largest Black festival of its kind has to be where it can grow, where it is valued, and where it serves the people who make it what it is. We need a partner that is committed to that growth and to the people it serves. ESSENCE has always evolved, and we are evolving in the best interest of our community. That is what Black women deserve.”

The station reported that New Orleans tourism officials are meeting with major festival producers as they look to launch a new festival after negotiations with Sundial Media over ESSENCE broke down over unresolved payment issues. In addition, the City of New Orleans issued a statement promising the upcoming announcement of a new festival.

“On December 9, the City of New Orleans and our tourism leaders will announce a new, world-class festival celebrating Black culture over the Fourth of July weekend,” read the city’s announcement. “We are partnering with some of the nation’s top Black entertainment producers who will bring major national talent to the Superdome while also showcasing our incredibly talented local artists and musicians.”

The letter from Essence noted that the 2027 event will take place elsewhere, but at press time organizers had not announced which city will host it next. “New Orleans, you helped make us who we are. And no matter where our journey takes us next, you’ll always be part of our story and in our hearts,” the farewell concluded.

Over the years, the festival has hosted everyone from LL Cool J to Usher, Lauryn Hill, D’Angelo, Destiny’s Child, Jill Scott, Al Green, Erykah Badu, Femi Kuti, New Edition, Jamie Foxx, Boyz II Men, Gary Clark Jr., Lizzo, Chance the Rapper, Bruno Mars and many more.

This year’s ESSENCE Festival featured sets from Cardi B, Kehlani, Latto, Patti LaBelle, Brandy and Monica, Leon Thomas, Babyface, Public Enemy, Doug E. Fresh, Big Boi and Big Freedia, among others.

Omneky Launches the Always-On Omneky Agent, an Autonomous Agent That Creates, Launches, and Continuously Improves Ad Campaigns

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

SAN FRANCISCO, Nov. 25, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Omneky, the data driven creative and advertising automation and optimization platform, today announced the Always-On AI Growth Agent, an autonomous AI agent that takes a campaign from brief to live ads, then keeps working around the clock: monitoring performance, refreshing creative, and updating campaigns based on results.

Marketers lose the hours between launch and optimization: creative goes stale, budgets spend against yesterday's performance, and small teams cannot watch every campaign. The agent closes that gap. A marketer provides a brief and brand assets; the agent develops the strategy, generates the creative, launches the campaign, and keeps improving it, with review and approval along the way.

The agent keeps persistent state for each brand, working from its products, assets, and history rather than starting over each session. Performance watchers monitor live campaigns and propose updates from results, a brand coordinator carries context between Slack and the web app, and a watchdog supervisor keeps the agents running reliably.

"Advertising does not stop when the workday ends, and neither should the work of improving it," said Hikari Senju, founder and CEO of Omneky. "The agent keeps learning from performance and keeps improving, while the marketer stays in control of the brief, the budget, and the brand."

In Omneky's TASTE BENCH harness evaluation (52 ad requests, 364 judged ads), Omneky scored 8.8 out of 10 on ad quality, ahead of the best competing tool at 7.6. More than $350 million of annualized ad spend runs through Omneky's systems today.

The Always-On AI Growth Agent is available now at www.omneky.com.

Omneky is a data driven creative and advertising automation and optimization platform. Founded in 2018 and headquartered in San Francisco, Omneky helps brands and agencies generate, launch, and optimize advertising creative at scale. For more information, visit www.omneky.com.

Apple has iPads running macOS already, but don’t get too excited

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

With iPads running the same chips as Macs, many have wished Apple would give us the option of running macOS on them instead of the somewhat clunky iPadOS windowing system. Apple’s previous arguments against converging devices have been somewhat weakened by the launch of the touchscreen MacBook Pro.

A new report says that Apple already has iPads running macOS internally, and that work on a giant iPad continues, but don’t hold your breath for a public launch of either …

As iPad hardware has grown more powerful over the years, the software has struggled to keep up. We saw split-screen capabilities first, with full windowing finally coming to the devices in iPadOS 26 last year.

The UI is still rather clunky compared to macOS, however, leading a significant number of people to wish that Apple simply offered the option of using the more powerful operating system on its tablet devices.

In a podcast Q&A, Bloomberg’s Mark Gurman says that Apple is already doing this internally, but there’s no sign of any plans to make it available outside the company.

Apple has iPads internally running macOS, and they’ve had that for some time. The fact that they have the underlying architecture of Apple Silicon that’s the same on the iPad and the Mac gives them the flexibility in order to test these types of things.

In terms if they’re actually going to do it – would they bring macOS to the iPad itself – at this point I don’t think that’s going to happen anytime soon. I’ve checked into this and there’s no ongoing operating system project right now in terms of a brand new operating system for the iPad – there is no work being done to consumerize macOs on the iPad.

Gurman says Apple seems set to continue to make piecemeal changes over time to bring iPad capabilities closer to those of the Mac but is showing no signs of full convergence.

He reiterated that work on a long-rumored giant-sized folding iPad continues.

The other thing I want to say is that I’m really excited for this potential of Project J312. That is, this giant 19-inch, 20-inch foldable iPad. And it’s basically like a gigantic iPhone Duo with no display on the outside.

However, he says there’s no guarantee it will ever launch, saying the timings have already slipped from 2027 through 2028 to 2029, but both cost and yield issues may still kill the project. If it does launch, it’s expected to cost anywhere from $3,000 to $5,000.
